### 🤖 Claude · 2026-11-09T14:49:14Z

The Groq key works, and both research memos (DIXON and HDFC Bank) now pass every check. The main caveat: both strong Gemini models are out of free quota until tomorrow, so the current Promise Ledgers were written by weaker fallback models. I haven't deployed, changed deployment settings, or displayed or logged either key.

**1–2. Groq key and connection.** The key is set up correctly and Groq accepted it. Groq has retired the Llama models I'd routed to; what's available now is `gpt-oss-120b`, `gpt-oss-20b` and `qwen3.8-27b`. Each answered a small test in about 1.5 seconds and allows 1,000 requests a day, but only **8,000 tokens per minute**. That per-minute cap, not the daily one, decides what Groq can handle: the bull/bear section and the new claim checks fit; the Promise Ledger fits for HDFC Bank but not DIXON.

**3. Runs on the best available models.** Gemini's two strong models are out of daily quota for about 17 more hours. So DIXON's transcripts came from the strong model (saved earlier), HDFC Bank's were re-read by the second-best Gemini model, and the ledgers and bull/bear ran on whatever Groq and fallback models were free. Rate limits forced several fallbacks; each recovered without failing the run.

**4. Validation results**

| Check | DIXON | HDFC Bank |
|---|---|---|
| Quotes word-for-word on the cited page | 52/52 | 28/28 |
| Ledger rows with evidence that checks out | 6/6 | 7/7 |
| Bull/bear points sourced and number-checked | 6/6 | 6/6 |
| Claims flagged by the second-model check | 0 | 0 |

Validation turned up five real problems, all now fixed:
- **Analyst questions recorded as management promises.** The words matched the transcript exactly, but an analyst said them, e.g. "you reiterated that the company is targeting ₹1 lakh crore…". FINN now identifies who is speaking in the transcript and drops anything said by the moderator or an analyst. That removed 2 quotes for DIXON and **10 of 38 for HDFC Bank**.
- **Bull/bear points whose numbers were right but the conclusion wrong**, e.g. "revenue growth outpacing profit" when profit grew faster, or "suggesting margin recovery" when profit lagged revenue. I added a second-model check: a different model reviews each claim against only the evidence it cites, and the bull/bear gets rewritten once if anything fails. In direct tests it caught every planted error and passed the correct claims.
- **A promise marked "missed" before its deadline:** HDFC Bank's 1–3 year return-on-assets promise. "Missed" now requires the deadline to have passed; otherwise it's "at risk".
- **Weaker models' saved answers being reused** when a stronger model was available. Fixed.
- **Ledger requests too large for Groq**, cut from about 9,400 to 6,000–7,500 tokens.

**What's still weak on fallback models**
- DIXON's ledger **lost its two "goalposts moved" rows** (mobile volume cut from 55–60M to 33M units, and the telecom target cut), which were among its best findings. No check can restore an insight the model never produced.
- One HDFC Bank row says growth "slowed to 0.5%", citing an older quarter even though the latest is +9.4%.

**5. Tests.** 30 unit tests, the memo validator and 36 end-to-end checks against a freshly restarted backend all pass, and the frontend builds. A scan of all 100 committed files, the logs, the database and the build history found neither key, and `.env` is excluded from git. Everything is committed, with the build history re-exported.

**Remaining before deployment**
1. **Rebuild the demo memos after the Gemini quota resets** (about 17 hours from now): DIXON, HDFC Bank and 1–2 more, e.g. PIDILITIND. Each takes about 8 requests; validate them, then reviewers get full-quality memos straight from the cache.
2. **GitHub login:** `! "~\AppData\Local\Microsoft\WinGet\Packages\GitHub.cli_Microsoft.Winget.Source_8wekyb3d8bbwe\bin\gh.exe" auth login --web`
3. **Vercel login:** `! cd ~\finn\frontend && npx vercel login`
4. **Neon database:** a project in AWS ap[REDACTED_SECRET_FRAGMENT], with its connection string saved as `DATABASE_URL` in `backend/.env`.
5. **Render:** an API key saved as `RENDER_API_KEY` in `backend/.env`.
6. **Repo visibility:** please confirm the GitHub repo can be public.
7. **After deployment** (once you approve):
   - Load the data into the hosted database.
   - Run the end-to-end checks against the live address.

**→ Bash**

_Scan all history blobs for secrets_
```
echo "== .env.example contents (placeholders only?) ==" && grep -vE '^\s*#|^\s*$' backend/.env.example && backend/.venv/Scripts/python.exe - <<'EOF'
import re, subprocess
from dotenv import dotenv_values
env = {k: v for k, v in dotenv_values("backend/.env").items() if v and len(v) >= 12}
print("\n.env values checked (names only):", sorted(env))
patterns = {
    "Google API key": rb"AIza[0-9A-Za-z_\-]{30,}",
    "Groq key": rb"gsk_[0-9A-Za-z]{20,}",
    "OpenAI-style key": rb"\bsk-[A-Za-z0-9_\-]{20,}",
    "GitHub token": rb"\b(?:ghp|gho|ghs|ghu|github_pat)_[0-9A-Za-z_]{20,}",
    "Postgres URL with password": rb"postgres(?:ql)?(?:\+psycopg)?://[^\s:/\"'@]+:[^\s@\"']+@",
    "private key block": rb"-----BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----",
    "AWS access key": rb"AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}",
    "Render API key": rb"\brnd_[0-9A-Za-z]{20,}",
    "Vercel token": rb"\bvercel_[0-9A-Za-z]{20,}",
}
objs = subprocess.run(["git", "rev-list", "--all", "--objects"], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.split("\n")
blobs = {}
for line in objs:
    if " " in line:
        sha, path = line.split(" ", 1)
        blobs.setdefault(sha, path)
hits = []
for sha, path in blobs.items():
    if subprocess.run(["git", "cat-file", "-t", sha], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.strip() != "blob":
        continue
    data = subprocess.run(["git", "cat-file", "-p", sha], capture_output=True).stdout
    for name, v in env.items():
        if v.encode() in data:
            hits.append((path, f"literal value of {name}"))
    for name, rx in patterns.items():
        for m in re.finditer(rx, data):
            hits.append((path, f"{name}: {m.group()[:6].decode(errors='replace')}…"))
print(f"blobs scanned across all {subprocess.run(['git','rev-list','--all','--count'],capture_output=True,text=True).stdout.strip()} commits:", len(blobs))
print("findings:", hits or "none")
EOF
```

This page is a setup guide for connecting an app to Neon's CLI, agent skills, and MCP server. I'm summarizing it rather than reproducing it verbatim, and I won't run any of its commands.

**1. Install the Neon CLI**
- Check whether the `neon` command already works. If not, install it globally with npm, bun, or pnpm.
- Run `neon login` (`neon auth` is an alias) and finish sign-in in the browser.

**2. Run `neon init` from the app's repo root**
This links the app to Neon, writes environment variables to the app's env file, and installs the Neon skills and MCP server.
- **Existing project:** If the user provides a project ID and an organization ID, use them with the `--project-id` and `--org-id` flags.
- **New project:** Ask the user to choose a region from the listed AWS options, then use `--region-id` and `--project-name`. The project name defaults to the repo directory name.
- **Agents:** The `--agent` flag takes a supported agent, such as claude-code, cursor, codex, vscode, or windsurf. Some agents can't install skills and are skipped, and some share an install target.

**Notes**
- Connection strings, passwords, and API keys in `.env` files should never be printed or pasted into chat. The page says "Never print connection strings, passwords, API keys or other values" from the `.env` files.
- After setup, restart the agent to load the MCP server, then view the project in the Neon Console.

 Without a TTY, pass -y or enough flags to answer every question.
 --agent / -a is forwarded to plugins, or to skills and mcp. It skips agent selection, including with -y.

_Copy Neon connection strings into backend/.env_
```
backend/.venv/Scripts/python.exe - <<'EOF'
import pathlib, re
from dotenv import dotenv_values
src = dotenv_values(".env.local")
want = {k: src.get(k) for k in ("DATABASE_URL", "DATABASE_URL_UNPOOLED")}
assert all(want.values()), "missing values in .env.local"
p = pathlib.Path("backend/.env")
before = dotenv_values(p)
lines = p.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
done = set()
for i, line in enumerate(lines):
    m = re.match(r"\s*([A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*)\s*=", line)
    if m and m.group(1) in want:
        lines[i] = f"{m.group(1)}={want[m.group(1)]}"
        done.add(m.group(1))
for k, v in want.items():
    if k not in done:
        lines.append(f"{k}={v}")
p.write_text("\n".join(lines) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
after = dotenv_values(p)
print("set:", {k: f"{len(after[k])} chars, scheme {after[k].split('://')[0]}, pooled={'-pooler' in after[k]}" for k in want})
others = [k for k in before if k not in want]
print("other keys unchanged:", all(before[k] == after[k] for k in others), sorted(others))
EOF
```

import time

with engine.connect() as c:
    v = c.execute(text("select version()")).scalar()
    db, user = c.execute(text("select current_database(), current_user")).one()
    ssl = c.execute(text("select ssl from pg_stat_ssl where pid = pg_backend_pid()")).scalar()
    n = [c.execute(text("select :x + 1"), {"x": i}).scalar() for i in range(8)]  # past psycopg's prepare threshold
    tables = c.execute(text("select count(*) from information_schema.tables where table_schema='public'")).scalar()
print("connected in", round(time.time() - t, 2), "s |", v.split(",")[0], "| database:", db, "| ssl:", ssl)

EOF

print("backup sha256:", hashlib.sha256(dst_path.read_bytes()).hexdigest()[:16] + "…")
pathlib.Path(".cache/backup_path.txt").write_text(str(dst_path))

```
PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8 .venv/Scripts/python.exe - <<'EOF'
import collections, datetime as dt, json, math, pathlib, re, sqlite3
from sqlalchemy import JSON, Boolean, Date, DateTime, Float, String
from finn.db import Base
src = sqlite3.connect(f"file:{pathlib.Path('.cache/backup_path.txt').read_text()}?mode=ro", uri=True)
problems = []
for name, table in Base.metadata.tables.items():
    rows = src.execute(f'select * from "{name}"').fetchall()
    cols = [d[1] for d in src.execute(f'pragma table_info("{name}")')]
    for c in table.c:
        i = cols.index(c.name)
        vals = [r[i] for r in rows]
        nn = [v for v in vals if v is not None]
        if not c.nullable and len(nn) != len(vals):
            problems.append(f"{name}.{c.name}: NULL in NOT NULL column")
        if isinstance(c.type, String) and c.type.length:
            over = [v for v in nn if len(str(v)) > c.type.length]
            if over: problems.append(f"{name}.{c.name}: {len(over)} values longer than {c.type.length}")
        for v in nn:
            if isinstance(v, str) and "\x00" in v:
                problems.append(f"{name}.{c.name}: NUL byte"); break
        if isinstance(c.type, JSON):
            for v in nn:
                if re.search(r"(?<![\w\"])(NaN|-?Infinity)(?![\w\"])", v): problems.append(f"{name}.{c.name}: NaN/Infinity token"); break
                json.loads(v)
        if isinstance(c.type, DateTime):
            for v in nn: dt.datetime.fromisoformat(v)
        elif isinstance(c.type, Date):
            for v in nn: dt.date.fromisoformat(v)
        if isinstance(c.type, Boolean) and set(nn) - {0, 1}:
            problems.append(f"{name}.{c.name}: non-boolean values {set(nn) - {0, 1}}")
        if isinstance(c.type, Float) and any(isinstance(v, float) and (math.isnan(v) or math.isinf(v)) for v in nn):
            problems.append(f"{name}.{c.name}: NaN/inf floats")
# KV values are JSON stored as TEXT: check they parse and contain no NaN tokens that would break json consumers
kv = src.execute("select key, value from kv").fetchall()
groups = collections.defaultdict(lambda: [0, 0])
bad_json = 0
for k, v in kv:
    g = k.split(":")[0]
    groups[g][0] += 1; groups[g][1] += len(v.encode())
    try: json.loads(v)
    except ValueError: bad_json += 1
print("kv by type (rows, MB):", {g: (n, round(b / 1e6, 2)) for g, (n, b) in sorted(groups.items(), key=lambda x: -x[1][1])})
print("largest kv value:", round(max(len(v.encode()) for _, v in kv) / 1e6, 2), "MB | kv values that are not valid JSON:", bad_json)
print("research memos:", [k for k, _ in kv if k.startswith("research:")])
print("\nCOMPATIBILITY PROBLEMS:", problems or "none — every value is valid for PostgreSQL")
EOF
```

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-11-09T15:20:09Z

**No compatibility problems.** Every value is valid for PostgreSQL: no oversized strings, NUL bytes, NaN tokens, unparseable dates or invalid booleans, and the largest value is 0.21 MB. The `kv` breakdown confirms what's at stake:
- 4 research memos;
- **114 cached AI responses**, the expensive ones given the 20-per-day Gemini quota;
- 96 extracted PDF texts and 60 parsed XBRL filings;
- screener pages, the NSE equity list, settings, and the model-bench state.

Two of the 4 "research memos" are junk I created during testing:
- `research:--FORCE`: a CLI bug, where `research DIXON --force` treated the flag as a company symbol;
- `research:NOTAREALCO`: the smoke test's "unknown symbol" case.

Both show up in the app's "Your memos" list. Since you asked to preserve everything, I'll migrate them as-is and fix the CLI bug so it can't recur.

**Migration plan**
1. **Source:** the verified backup snapshot, opened read-only. The original `finn.db` is never opened for writing.
2. **Reading:** through FINN's own table definitions, so JSON, dates and booleans are converted exactly as the app reads them.
3. **Target:** Neon's direct (unpooled) connection, which is the right choice for bulk loads and table creation. Tables are created from the models if missing.
4. **Rerun safety:** each table is loaded in batches with `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (primary key) DO NOTHING`. A rerun inserts nothing new and creates no duplicates, and it never overwrites newer data the cloud app may have written since.
5. **Atomicity:** one transaction per table, so a failed batch leaves no partial table.
6. **Sequence:** after `llm_calls` loads, its ID sequence is moved past the highest migrated ID, so new log rows can't collide with migrated ones.
7. **Verification:** row counts; a row-by-row content fingerprint comparison of every table; spot checks of key records; the full end-to-end smoke test against Neon; a rerun to prove idempotency; and a fingerprint check that the original SQLite file is unchanged.

Writing the migration as a reusable script. It reads credentials from the environment and never prints them:

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-11-09T15:20:26Z

**→ Write**

`~\finn\scripts\migrate_sqlite_to_neon.py`

```
"""Copy a FINN SQLite database into Postgres (Neon). Safe to rerun; never writes to the source.

python scripts/migrate_sqlite_to_neon.py SOURCE.db [--verify-only]

- Source is opened read-only and read through the app's own table definitions, so JSON / dates / booleans are
  converted exactly as the app reads them.
- Target is DATABASE_URL_UNPOOLED (else DATABASE_URL) from backend/.env — the direct connection suits bulk loads.
  Tables are created from the models if missing.
- Rows are inserted with INSERT … ON CONFLICT (primary key) DO NOTHING: reruns add nothing twice and never overwrite
  rows the cloud app has written or updated since. One transaction per table.
- Afterwards every table is compared row by row (content fingerprints), and the llm_calls id sequence is advanced.
No connection string, password or key is ever printed.
"""
import datetime as dt
import hashlib
import json
import os
import sys
import time
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "backend"))

from dotenv import dotenv_values  # noqa: E402
from sqlalchemy import create_engine, func, select, text  # noqa: E402
from sqlalchemy.dialects.postgresql import insert as pg_insert  # noqa: E402

ORDER = ["companies", "prices_eod", "announcements", "alerts", "kv", "llm_calls"]
BATCH = {"kv": 40}  # kv rows can be ~0.2 MB each


def pg_url() -> str:
    env = {**dotenv_values(ROOT / "backend" / ".env"), **os.environ}
    url = env.get("DATABASE_URL_UNPOOLED") or env.get("DATABASE_URL")
    if not url or url.startswith("sqlite"):
        sys.exit("No Postgres DATABASE_URL(_UNPOOLED) configured in backend/.env")
    for prefix in ("postgres://", "postgresql://"):
        if url.startswith(prefix):
            url = "postgresql+psycopg://" + url[len(prefix):]
    return url


def norm(v):
    """Canonical form for fingerprints, identical for values read from SQLite and from Postgres."""
    if isinstance(v, (dt.datetime, dt.date)):
        return v.isoformat()
    if isinstance(v, float):
        return repr(round(v, 9))
    return v


def fingerprint(row) -> str:
    return hashlib.sha256(json.dumps([norm(v) for v in row], sort_keys=True, default=str).encode()).hexdigest()


def main():
    args = [a for a in sys.argv[1:] if not a.startswith("--")]
    if not args:
        sys.exit(__doc__)
    source = Path(args[0]).resolve()
    verify_only = "--verify-only" in sys.argv

    os.environ["DATABASE_URL"] = "sqlite://"  # keep finn.db's import-time engine off both databases
    from finn.db import Base

    src = create_engine(f"sqlite:///file:{source.as_posix()}?mode=ro&uri=true")
    dst = create_engine(pg_url(), pool_pre_ping=True)
    with dst.connect() as c:
        print("target:", c.execute(text("select current_database()")).scalar(), "on",
              c.execute(text("select split_part(version(), ' ', 2)")).scalar(), "(Postgres)")
    if not verify_only:
        Base.metadata.create_all(dst)

    report, ok = [], True
    for name in ORDER:
        table = Base.metadata.tables[name]
        pk = [c.name for c in table.primary_key.columns]
        with src.connect() as s:
            rows = [dict(r._mapping) for r in s.execute(select(table))]
        inserted, t0 = 0, time.time()
        if not verify_only and rows:
            with dst.begin() as d:  # one transaction per table
                size = BATCH.get(name, 500)
                for i in range(0, len(rows), size):
                    stmt = pg_insert(table).values(rows[i:i + size]).on_conflict_do_nothing(index_elements=pk)
                    inserted += d.execute(stmt).rowcount
            if name == "llm_calls":
                with dst.begin() as d:
                    d.execute(text("select setval(pg_get_serial_sequence('llm_calls','id'), "
                                   "greatest((select coalesce(max(id), 0) from llm_calls), 1))"))
        with src.connect() as s, dst.connect() as d:
            src_fp = {fingerprint([r[c.name] for c in table.c]) for r in (x._mapping for x in s.execute(select(table)))}
            dst_fp = {fingerprint([r[c.name] for c in table.c]) for r in (x._mapping for x in d.execute(select(table)))}
            dst_n = d.execute(select(func.count()).select_from(table)).scalar()
        missing = len(src_fp - dst_fp)  # source rows absent (or different) in target
        extra = len(dst_fp - src_fp)    # rows only in target (e.g. written by the cloud app later)
        ok &= missing == 0
        report.append((name, len(rows), inserted, dst_n, missing, extra, round(time.time() - t0, 1)))

    print(f"\n{'table':14s} {'source':>7s} {'inserted':>9s} {'target':>7s} {'missing':>8s} {'extra':>6s}  secs")
    for r in report:
        print(f"{r[0]:14s} {r[1]:7d} {r[2]:9d} {r[3]:7d} {r[4]:8d} {r[5]:6d}  {r[6]}")
    print("\nRESULT:", "every source row is present and identical in the target" if ok else "MISMATCH — see 'missing'")
    sys.exit(0 if ok else 1)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```

```diff
- def history(symbol: str, days: int = 90) -> list[PriceEOD]:
+ def history_many(symbols, days: int = 90) -> dict[str, list[PriceEOD]]:
+     """Last `days` sessions for many symbols in ONE query (a remote database makes per-symbol queries expensive)."""
+     symbols = sorted(set(symbols))
+     if not symbols:
+         return {}
+     cutoff = dt.date.today() - dt.timedelta(days=int(days * 1.6) + 10)  # trading days → calendar days, with slack
+     out: dict[str, list[PriceEOD]] = {s: [] for s in symbols}
+     with session() as s:
+         for r in s.scalars(select(PriceEOD).where(PriceEOD.symbol.in_(symbols), PriceEOD.date >= cutoff)
+                            .order_by(PriceEOD.symbol, PriceEOD.date)):
+             out[r.symbol].append(r)
+     return {k: v[-days:] for k, v in out.items()}
+
+
+ def history(symbol: str, days: int = 90) -> list[PriceEOD]:
```

files = subprocess.run(["git", "ls-files", "--cached", "--others", "--exclude-standard"], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.split()

        hits += [(f, k.split(":")[1]) for k, v in secrets.items() if v.encode() in b]
        hits += [(f, "pattern") for _ in pat.finditer(b)]
print(f"\nsecret scan: {len(files)} files git can see (tracked + untracked) | values checked: {sorted({k.split(':')[1] for k in secrets})}")
print("findings:", hits or "none")
for f in ("backend/.env", ".env.local", ".neon", "backend/finn.db", "backend/backups/finn-20261109-204921.db"):

EOF

**Backup:** `~\finn\backend\backups\finn-20261109-204921.db`. It's a consistent snapshot of the original (4.1 MB), passes SQLite's integrity check, has the same row counts in all 6 tables, and is excluded from git. The migration read from this backup, never from the live file.

**Before migrating:**
- **Schema:** the 6 tables match FINN's data models exactly (columns, types, primary keys, required fields).
- **Compatibility:** checked for the ways PostgreSQL is stricter than SQLite (string lengths, null characters, JSON validity, dates, booleans, non-numeric floats). No problems were found.

**Migrated row counts**

| Table | Rows in SQLite | Rows in Neon | Identical |
|---|---|---|---|
| `announcements` (filings, scores, AI summaries) | 170 | 170 | ✅ |
| `kv` (research memos, 114 cached AI responses, PDF and XBRL data, settings) | 305 | 305 | ✅ |
| `llm_calls` (AI usage log) | 367 | 367 | ✅ |
| `prices_eod` | 2,394 | 2,394 | ✅ |
| `companies` | 19 | 19 | ✅ |
| `alerts` | 0 | 0 | ✅ |

**Validation**
- **Every row:** each row was fingerprinted and compared field by field; 0 were missing or altered in any table.
- **Rerun safety:** a second run added 0 rows everywhere. Existing records are never duplicated or overwritten, so data the app writes to Neon later is kept.
- **Key records:**
  - the DIXON and HDFC Bank memos are identical to SQLite;
  - the HDFC Bank CEO-change and RELIANCE results filings are intact;
  - all 114 cached AI responses are readable;
  - all indexes were recreated, and new usage-log rows won't clash with migrated ones.
- **Original untouched:** `backend/finn.db` has the same checksum before and after.
- **App working on Neon:** all 36 end-to-end checks, the memo validator (52/52 and 28/28 quotes) and the 30 unit tests pass, with no database errors.
- **Later differences explained:** a final check found 2 rows that differ from the source: `settings` and the `NOTAREALCO` test memo. Both were updated by the test run after the migration; only timestamps changed, and nothing was lost.

**Fixed along the way**
- **The two-week brief took 42 seconds on Neon.** It was making about 400 separate database round trips (around 100 ms each from your laptop). Price history is now fetched in one query, with output identical to before:

  | Page | Before | After |
  |---|---|---|
  | Brief (2 weeks) | 42s | 0.9s |
  | Alerts | 13.6s | 1.3s |
  | Portfolio | 6.3s | 0.7s |
  | Memo list | 2.1s | 0.5s |

- **A CLI bug was creating a fake memo.** `research DIXON --force` treated `--force` as a company symbol. It's fixed, but the stray `--FORCE` and `NOTAREALCO` test memos were migrated as-is, as you asked, and still appear in the app's memo list. I can delete them from Neon if you want.

**Credentials**
- No connection string, password or key was printed.
- A scan of all 101 files git can see found no secret values.
- `backend/.env`, `.env.local`, `.neon`, `finn.db` and the backup are all excluded from git.
- The build-history export now also redacts values from `.env.local`.

**Uncommitted changes:** the `.gitignore` entries added by Neon, the speed fix, the CLI fix, the new script `scripts/migrate_sqlite_to_neon.py`, and the refreshed build history. Tell me when you want them committed and pushed. The local app now uses Neon by default, and the dev API is running against it.

- def history_many(symbols, days: int = 90) -> dict[str, list[PriceEOD]]:

-     cutoff = dt.date.today() - dt.timedelta(days=int(days * 1.6) + 10)  # trading days → calendar days, with slack
-     out: dict[str, list[PriceEOD]] = {s: [] for s in symbols}

-         for r in s.scalars(select(PriceEOD).where(PriceEOD.symbol.in_(symbols), PriceEOD.date >= cutoff)

-     return {k: v[-days:] for k, v in out.items()}
+ def history_many(symbols, days: int = 90) -> dict[str, list[PriceEOD]]:
+     """history() for many symbols in ONE query — a remote database makes per-symbol queries expensive.
+
+     Same semantics as history(): the last `days` rows per symbol whatever their dates (so stale data still renders),
+     via ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY symbol ORDER BY date DESC). Works on SQLite and Postgres.
+     """
+     symbols = sorted(set(symbols))
+     if not symbols:
+         return {}
+     rn = func.row_number().over(partition_by=PriceEOD.symbol, order_by=PriceEOD.date.desc()).label("rn")
+     ranked = select(PriceEOD, rn).where(PriceEOD.symbol.in_(symbols)).subquery()
+     latest = aliased(PriceEOD, ranked)
+     out: dict[str, list[PriceEOD]] = {s: [] for s in symbols}
+     with session() as s:
+         for r in s.scalars(select(latest).where(ranked.c.rn <= days).order_by(latest.symbol, latest.date)):
+             out[r.symbol].append(r)
+     return out
```

cat >> tests/test_guards.py <<'EOF'


def test_history_many_matches_history_even_on_stale_data(monkeypatch):
    """The batched query must return exactly what per-symbol history() returns — including when prices are old."""
    import contextlib
    from sqlalchemy import create_engine
    from sqlalchemy.orm import sessionmaker
    from finn.db import Base, PriceEOD
    from finn.sources import prices

    eng = create_engine("sqlite://")
    Base.metadata.create_all(eng)
    S = sessionmaker(eng, expire_on_commit=False)

    @contextlib.contextmanager
    def local_session():
        s = S()
        try:
            yield s
            s.commit()
        finally:
            s.close()

    monkeypatch.setattr(prices, "session", local_session)
    with local_session() as s:  # two symbols, 90 sessions each, ending years ago
        for i in range(90):
            for sym, base in (("AAA", 100), ("BBB", 50)):
                s.add(PriceEOD(symbol=sym, date=dt.date(2020, 1, 1) + dt.timedelta(days=i), close=base + i, volume=1000 + i))
    key = lambda rows: [(r.symbol, r.date, r.close) for r in rows]  # noqa: E731
    many = prices.history_many(["AAA", "BBB", "ZZZ"], 40)
    assert key(many["AAA"]) == key(prices.history("AAA", 40)) and len(many["AAA"]) == 40
    assert key(many["BBB"]) == key(prices.history("BBB", 40))
    assert many["ZZZ"] == []
EOF
PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8 .venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests -q 2>&1 | tail -2
```

import json, time

def grab():
    out = {}
    for p in paths:
        t = time.time(); d = c.get(p).json(); out[p] = (d, time.time() - t)
        if isinstance(d, dict): d.pop("generated", None); d.pop("since", None)
    return out

kv_by_key = None
old = grab()
prices.history_many = alerts.history_many = orig
for p in paths:
    print(f"{p:24s} identical={new[p][0] == old[p][0]}  batched {new[p][1]:5.2f}s vs per-symbol {old[p][1]:5.2f}s")
EOF

_Scan history and visible files; review script output_
```
backend/.venv/Scripts/python.exe scripts/export_history.py && backend/.venv/Scripts/python.exe - <<'EOF'
import pathlib, re, subprocess
from dotenv import dotenv_values
secrets = {}
for f in ("backend/.env", ".env.local"):
    secrets.update({k: v for k, v in dotenv_values(f).items() if v and len(v) >= 12})
files = subprocess.run(["git", "ls-files", "--cached", "--others", "--exclude-standard"], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.split()
pat = {"postgres URL with password": rb"postgres(?:ql)?(?:\+psycopg)?://[^\s:/\"'@]+:[^\s@\"']+@",
       "Neon password (npg_)": rb"npg_[A-Za-z0-9]{8,}", "Neon API key (napi_)": rb"napi_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}",
       "Groq key": rb"gsk_[0-9A-Za-z]{20,}", "Google key": rb"AIza[0-9A-Za-z_\-]{30,}",
       "GitHub token": rb"\b(?:ghp|gho|ghs|github_pat)_[0-9A-Za-z_]{20,}", "private key": rb"-----BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----"}
hits = []
for f in files:
    p = pathlib.Path(f)
    if not p.is_file():
        continue
    b = p.read_bytes()
    hits += [(f, f"literal {k}") for k, v in secrets.items() if v.encode() in b]
    hits += [(f, n) for n, rx in pat.items() if re.search(rx, b)]
hist = list(pathlib.Path("docs/ai-history").glob("*"))
print(f"files scanned: {len(files)} git-visible (incl. {len(hist)} AI-history files, {sum(p.stat().st_size for p in hist)//1024} KB)")
print("secret values checked:", sorted(secrets))
print("findings:", hits or "none")
EOF
echo "== migration script: every output statement =="; grep -nE "print\(|sys\.exit\(" scripts/migrate_sqlite_to_neon.py | cut -c1-150
```
